=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array of integers nums.
Your task is to find the length of the longest subsequence seq of nums, such that the absolute differences between consecutive elements form a non-increasing sequence of integers. In other words, for a subsequence seq_0, seq_1, seq_2, ..., seq_m of nums, |seq_1 - seq_0| >= |seq_2 - seq_1| >= ... >= |seq_m - seq_m - 1|.
Return the length of such a subsequence.
A subsequence is an non-empty array that can be derived from another array by deleting some or no elements without changing the order of the remaining elements.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [16,6,3]
Output: 3
Explanation: 
The longest subsequence is [16, 6, 3] with the absolute adjacent differences [10, 3].

Example 2:

Input: nums = [6,5,3,4,2,1]
Output: 4
Explanation:
The longest subsequence is [6, 4, 2, 1] with the absolute adjacent differences [2, 2, 1].

Example 3:

Input: nums = [10,20,10,19,10,20]
Output: 5
Explanation: 
The longest subsequence is [10, 20, 10, 19, 10] with the absolute adjacent differences [10, 10, 9, 9].

 
Constraints:

2 <= nums.length <= 10^4
1 <= nums[i] <= 300

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def longestSubsequence(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's draft code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestSubsequence(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        MAX_VAL = 300
        INF = 301
        D = INF  # actual diff indices 0..299, INF index for singleton
        dp = [[0] * (D + 1) for _ in range(MAX_VAL + 1)]
        suf = [[0] * (D + 1) for _ in range(MAX_VAL + 1)]
        ans = 0
        for x in nums:
            updates = [0] * (D + 1)
            # compute transitions from old states
            for v in range(1, MAX_VAL + 1):
                d = abs(x - v)
                best = suf[v][d]
                if best:
                    val = best + 1
                    if val > updates[d]:
                        updates[d] = val
            # singleton ending at x
            if dp[x][D] == 0:
                dp[x][D] = 1
            # apply updates
            row = dp[x]
            for d in range(D):  # 0..300
                if updates[d] > row[d]:
                    row[d] = updates[d]
            # recompute suffix for row x
            srow = suf[x]
            srow[D] = row[D]
            for d in range(D - 1, -1, -1):
                # srow[d] = max(row[d], srow[d+1])
                nxt = srow[d + 1]
                if row[d] >= nxt:
                    srow[d] = row[d]
                else:
                    srow[d] = nxt
            if srow[0] > ans:
                ans = srow[0]
        return ans
```

Let's reason through a tricky case where max length suffix chooses a subsequence with last diff >= d but not necessarily the one that yields best future after appending x? After appending x, the new state's last diff is d, independent of previous last diff. The length is all that matters for future? Wait, future after appending x: the subsequence now ends at x with last diff d. Its length is `best+1`. For future transitions from x with new diff d2, the condition will be d >= d2. The previous history (earlier diffs) is non-increasing and all >= d (because previous last diff d_prev >= d and earlier diffs >= d_prev >= d). So the only relevant property is last diff d and length. Thus among all subsequences ending at v with last diff >= d, the longest one gives the best new state for a given d. There is no trade-off where a shorter subsequence with much larger previous last diff could be better after appending x, because after appending x, the previous last diff is replaced by d; the earlier diffs of the shorter subsequence are at least its previous last diff (which was >= d), so they are also >= d. The new state's last diff is d. The length is shorter, so never better. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `srow[D] = row[D]`; if D
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestSubsequence(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        max_val = max(nums)
        inf = max_val + 1          # represents a length-1 subsequence with no previous diff
        D = inf

        # dp[v][d] = best length ending with value v and last difference exactly d
        dp = [[0] * (D + 1) for _ in range(max_val + 1)]

        # suf[v][d] = max(dp[v][k]) for k >= d
        suf = [[0] * (D + 1) for _ in range(max_val + 1)]

        ans = 0

        for x in nums:
            updates = [0] * (D + 1)

            # Extend old subsequences to current value x.
            for v in range(max_val + 1):
                d = abs(x - v)
                best = suf[v][d]
                if best:
                    cand = best + 1
                    if cand > updates[d]:
                        updates[d] = cand

            # A single element subsequence ending at x.
            if dp[x][D] == 0:
                dp[x][D] = 1

            row = dp[x]
            for d in range(D):
                if updates[d] > row[d]:
                    row[d] = updates[d]

            # Rebuild suffix maxima for row x.
            srow = suf[x]
            srow[D] = row[D]
            for d in range(D - 1, -1, -1):
                if row[d] >= srow[d + 1]:
                    srow[d] = row[d]
                else:
                    srow[d] = srow[d + 1]

            if srow[0] > ans:
                ans = srow[0]

        return ans
```
